It’s… clear enough that I answer them sometimes, and then realize no one else heard anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like people are plotting against you or that certain events in your life are specially directed at you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… plotting is a strong word. Sometimes things feel pointed at me, but I’m not sure. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Are these voices you hear something that happens every day, multiple times a day, or do they come and go depending on your stress or situation?\n\nPatient: It comes and goes, I guess… mostly at work. It’s enough that I hear it through the conference room walls, or from my phone even when it’s off. Usually it’s board members or staff saying I’m ruining things.\n\nIt sounds real at the time. Clear enough that I… answer sometimes, before I realize no one else heard it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts or beliefs that others don’t seem to share, even when there’s no voices involved?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean… maybe sometimes things feel off, like people don’t see what I’m seeing, but without the voices… I don’t know. I haven’t really thought about it that way.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you believe something is true that others around you don’t seem to agree with, even when there’s no voices involved?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. Maybe, but I couldn’t point to something specific. It’s more just a feeling sometimes, and then I second-guess it.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you've believed something strongly to be true — like people being against you or things being directed at you — even when others didn’t agree or couldn’t see what you were talking about?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not like that, exactly. I mean, I can get the feeling people are looking at me or talking around me, but I can usually talk myself out of it after a while.\n\nI wouldn’t say I… strongly believe it, no. Not without something happening.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you've held onto a belief that something was real — like people being against you or things being directed at you — even when others clearly didn’t see it the same way, and there were no voices involved at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not if there weren’t voices or something else going on. I might feel uneasy, but I don’t know that I’d say I held onto a belief like that. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a strong belief that something was happening or that someone was doing something specific toward you — like spying, sabotaging, or plotting — that you couldn’t let go of, even for a period of time, and without hearing voices or other things happening?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like spying or plotting. If I got worried about something, it usually wasn’t… solid like that. I’d question it. I don’t think I held onto it without something else going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you truly believed something was happening—like being targeted, followed, or talked about—so strongly that no one could convince you otherwise, even when there were no voices or other experiences influencing that belief?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Not that strongly… if there weren’t other things going on, I usually could still wonder if I was wrong. I don’t remember a time where nobody could talk me out of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you believed something was definitely true — like someone was trying to harm you, trick you, or send you a message — and you were completely sure of it, even when no one else could hear anything or confirm what you believed?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had it like that. If nobody else could confirm it, I’d usually be unsure, or I’d keep it to myself. I don’t remember being completely sure in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something unusual was definitely happening — like being tricked, followed, or targeted — and it felt so real to you that you couldn’t shake the idea, even when you were alone and there were no voices or other things influencing that belief?\n\nPatient: No… not by itself. I mean, I can get uneasy about things, but if I’m alone and there’s nothing else going on, I don’t think I’ve been that certain. I usually still have doubt.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you strongly believed something unusual was definitely happening — like being targeted or tricked — that lasted for weeks or months, even after the voices went away or when there was nothing else strange happening?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not for weeks or months after everything else had stopped. If there wasn’t anything else happening, I don’t think I’d stay that convinced.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you here?\n\nPatient: I… I’m here because I’ve been hearing people talking about me. At work, mostly. Through the conference room walls, or sometimes from my phone even when it’s off.\n\nIt sounds like board members or staff saying I’m ruining the company. It’s… clear enough that I answer them sometimes, and then realize no one else heard anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like people are plotting against you or that certain events in your life are specially directed at you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… plotting is a strong word. Sometimes things feel pointed at me, but I’m not sure. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Are these voices you hear something that happens every day, multiple times a day, or do they come and go depending on your stress or situation?\n\nPatient: It comes and goes, I guess… mostly at work. It’s enough that I hear it through the conference room walls, or from my phone even when it’s off. Usually it’s board members or staff saying I’m ruining things.\n\nIt sounds real at the time. Clear enough that I… answer sometimes, before I realize no one else heard it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts or beliefs that others don’t seem to share, even when there’s no voices involved?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean… maybe sometimes things feel off, like people don’t see what I’m seeing, but without the voices… I don’t know. I haven’t really thought about it that way.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you believe something is true that others around you don’t seem to agree with, even when there’s no voices involved?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. Maybe, but I couldn’t point to something specific. It’s more just a feeling sometimes, and then I second-guess it.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you've believed something strongly to be true — like people being against you or things being directed at you — even when others didn’t agree or couldn’t see what you were talking about?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not like that, exactly. I mean, I can get the feeling people are looking at me or talking around me, but I can usually talk myself out of it after a while.\n\nI wouldn’t say I… strongly believe it, no. Not without something happening.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you've held onto a belief that something was real — like people being against you or things being directed at you — even when others clearly didn’t see it the same way, and there were no voices involved at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not if there weren’t voices or something else going on. I might feel uneasy, but I don’t know that I’d say I held onto a belief like that. Not that strongly… if there weren’t other things going on, I usually could still wonder if I was wrong. I don’t remember a time where nobody could talk me out of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you believed something was definitely true — like someone was trying to harm you, trick you, or send you a message — and you were completely sure of it, even when no one else could hear anything or confirm what you believed?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had it like that. If nobody else could confirm it, I’d usually be unsure, or I’d keep it to myself. I don’t remember being completely sure in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something unusual was definitely happening — like being tricked, followed, or targeted — and it felt so real to you that you couldn’t shake the idea, even when you were alone and there were no voices or other things influencing that belief?\n\nPatient: No… not by itself. I mean… if things quieted down, I don’t remember still holding onto something like that for days or weeks. Not in a way I couldn’t shake.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that something was happening—like being deceived, followed, or targeted—that seemed completely real to you even though others didn’t share that belief, and this belief lasted for a long time, even when you weren’t hearing any voices or experiencing anything unusual at the time?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Are these voices you hear something that happens every day, multiple times a day, or do they come and go depending on your stress or situation?\n\nPatient: It comes and goes, I guess… mostly at work. It’s enough that I hear it through the conference room walls, or from my phone even when it’s off. Usually it’s board members or staff saying I’m ruining things.\n\nIt sounds real at the time. Clear enough that I… answer sometimes, before I realize no one else heard it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts or beliefs that others don’t seem to share, even when there’s no voices involved?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean… maybe sometimes things feel off, like people don’t see what I’m seeing, but without the voices… I don’t know. I haven’t really thought about it that way.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you believe something is true that others around you don’t seem to agree with, even when there’s no voices involved?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. It’s… clear enough that I answer them sometimes, and then realize no one else heard anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like people are plotting against you or that certain events in your life are specially directed at you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… plotting is a strong word. Sometimes things feel pointed at me, but I’m not sure. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Are these voices you hear something that happens every day, multiple times a day, or do they come and go depending on your stress or situation?\n\nPatient: It comes and goes, I guess… mostly at work. It’s enough that I hear it through the conference room walls, or from my phone even when it’s off. Usually it’s board members or staff saying I’m ruining things.\n\nIt sounds real at the time. Clear enough that I… answer sometimes, before I realize no one else heard it.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts or beliefs that others don’t seem to share, even when there’s no voices involved?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I mean… maybe sometimes things feel off, like people don’t see what I’m seeing, but without the voices… I don’t know. I haven’t really thought about it that way.\n\nDoctor: Are there times when you believe something is true that others around you don’t seem to agree with, even when there’s no voices involved?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t know. Maybe, but I couldn’t point to something specific. It’s more just a feeling sometimes, and then I second-guess it.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you've believed something strongly to be true — like people being against you or things being directed at you — even when others didn’t agree or couldn’t see what you were talking about?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not like that, exactly. I mean, I can get the feeling people are looking at me or talking around me, but I can usually talk myself out of it after a while.\n\nI wouldn’t say I… strongly believe it, no. Not without something happening.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you've held onto a belief that something was real — like people being against you or things being directed at you — even when others clearly didn’t see it the same way, and there were no voices involved at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not if there weren’t voices or something else going on. I might feel uneasy, but I don’t know that I’d say I held onto a belief like that. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a strong belief that something was happening or that someone was doing something specific toward you — like spying, sabotaging, or plotting — that you couldn’t let go of, even for a period of time, and without hearing voices or other things happening?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like spying or plotting. If I got worried about something, it usually wasn’t… solid like that. I’d question it. I don’t think I held onto it without something else going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you truly believed something was happening—like being targeted, followed, or talked about—so strongly that no one could convince you otherwise, even when there were no voices or other experiences influencing that belief?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Not that strongly… if there weren’t other things going on, I usually could still wonder if I was wrong. I don’t remember a time where nobody could talk me out of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you believed something was definitely true — like someone was trying to harm you, trick you, or send you a message — and you were completely sure of it, even when no one else could hear anything or confirm what you believed?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had it like that. If nobody else could confirm it, I’d usually be unsure, or I’d keep it to myself. I don’t remember being completely sure in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something unusual was definitely happening — like being tricked, followed, or targeted — and it felt so real to you that you couldn’t shake the idea, even when you were alone and there were no voices or other things influencing that belief?\n\nPatient: No… not by itself. I mean, I can get uneasy about things, but if I’m alone and there’s nothing else going on, I don’t think I’ve been that certain. I usually still have doubt.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you strongly believed something unusual was definitely happening — like being targeted or tricked — that lasted for weeks or months, even after the voices went away or when there was nothing else strange happening?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not if nothing else was happening at the time. I might wonder about things, but I don’t think it stayed completely real to me like that for a long time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you believed something strongly—like being targeted or tricked—without hearing any voices or experiencing anything strange, and that belief stayed with you for days or longer?\n\nPatient: No… not really. If there wasn’t anything else going on, I don’t think I’d hold onto it like that for days. Maybe I’d feel unsure, but not strongly believe it.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something strongly—like being targeted or tricked—without hearing any voices or experiencing anything strange, and that belief stayed with you for several hours or more?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Maybe, but I couldn’t point to something specific. It’s more just a feeling sometimes, and then I second-guess it.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you've believed something strongly to be true — like people being against you or things being directed at you — even when others didn’t agree or couldn’t see what you were talking about?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not like that, exactly. I mean, I can get the feeling people are looking at me or talking around me, but I can usually talk myself out of it after a while.\n\nI wouldn’t say I… strongly believe it, no. Not without something happening.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you've held onto a belief that something was real — like people being against you or things being directed at you — even when others clearly didn’t see it the same way, and there were no voices involved at all?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not if there weren’t voices or something else going on. I might feel uneasy, but I don’t know that I’d say I held onto a belief like that. It’s hard to explain.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a strong belief that something was happening or that someone was doing something specific toward you — like spying, sabotaging, or plotting — that you couldn’t let go of, even for a period of time, and without hearing voices or other things happening?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not like spying or plotting. If I got worried about something, it usually wasn’t… solid like that. I’d question it. I don’t think I held onto it without something else going on.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period where you truly believed something was happening—like being targeted, followed, or talked about—so strongly that no one could convince you otherwise, even when there were no voices or other experiences influencing that belief?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. Not that strongly… if there weren’t other things going on, I usually could still wonder if I was wrong. I don’t remember a time where nobody could talk me out of it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you believed something was definitely true — like someone was trying to harm you, trick you, or send you a message — and you were completely sure of it, even when no one else could hear anything or confirm what you believed?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had it like that. If nobody else could confirm it, I’d usually be unsure, or I’d keep it to myself. I don’t remember being completely sure in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something unusual was definitely happening — like being tricked, followed, or targeted — and it felt so real to you that you couldn’t shake the idea, even when you were alone and there were no voices or other things influencing that belief?\n\nPatient: No… not by itself. I mean, I can get uneasy about things, but if I’m alone and there’s nothing else going on, I don’t think I’ve been that certain. I usually still have doubt.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you strongly believed something unusual was definitely happening — like being targeted or tricked — that lasted for weeks or months, even after the voices went away or when there was nothing else strange happening?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not for weeks or months after everything else had stopped. If there wasn’t anything else happening, I don’t think I’d stay that convinced.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you were completely convinced something unusual was happening—like being targeted or tricked—and even after the voices stopped or when nothing else was going on, you still couldn't shake that belief for days, weeks, or longer?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean… if things quieted down, I don’t remember still holding onto something like that for days or weeks. Not in a way I couldn’t shake.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that something was happening—like being deceived, followed, or targeted—that seemed completely real to you even though others didn’t share that belief, and this belief lasted for a long time, even when you weren’t hearing any voices or experiencing anything unusual at the time?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not if nothing else was happening at the time. I might wonder about things, but I don’t think it stayed completely real to me like that for a long time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you believed something strongly—like being targeted or tricked—without hearing any voices or experiencing anything strange, and that belief stayed with you for days or longer?\n\nPatient: No… not really. If there wasn’t anything else going on, I don’t think I’d hold onto it like that for days. Maybe I’d feel unsure, but not strongly believe it.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you believed something strongly—like being targeted or tricked—without hearing any voices or experiencing anything strange, and that belief stayed with you for several hours or more?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, maybe I’ve gotten uneasy for a little while, but not like… strongly believing it for hours when nothing else was happening.\n\nDoctor: Have there been moments when you believed something was definitely happening—like being targeted or deceived—that felt completely real to you, even though there were no voices or anything strange happening at the time, and this belief lasted for an hour or more?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. Not for an hour like that. If nothing else was happening, I’d probably just feel uneasy and then let it go.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you truly believed something unusual was happening—like being targeted or deceived—and that belief stayed with you for some time, even when you weren’t hearing any voices or experiencing anything strange?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not on its own like that. I might get suspicious for a bit, but if nothing else is happening, it doesn’t really stay with me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever believed something that others might find strange or unbelievable—like having special powers, being part of a secret mission, or being transformed in some way—and felt that it was definitely true, even when no one else agreed and there were no voices involved at all?\n\nPatient: No… not like special powers or a secret mission. I don’t think I’ve had anything like that without something else going on."
